> AGENT

The path `argocd/applications` in the SETUP.md is wrong. The Application manifest needs to be applied to the `argocd` **namespace** (line 5: `namespace: argocd`), but the file itself lives at `environments/dev/argocd/application.yaml`.

The correct `kubectl apply` command is:

```bash
kubectl apply -f environments/dev/argocd/application.yaml
```

The `-n argocd` flag isn't even needed since the namespace is already specified in the manifest. But the error you're getting means ArgoCD itself isn't installed on the cluster - the `Application` CRD doesn't exist. That's a platform-level dependency, not a path issue.

> AGENT

ArgoCD isn't installed on the dev GKE cluster. The `Application` CRD doesn't exist because ArgoCD hasn't been deployed there yet.

This is managed by the platform team via kix-platform-infrastructure's `argocd` scope (`environments/dev/kubernetes/argocd/`). You'll need to either:

1. **Ask the platform team** to deploy ArgoCD to the dev cluster
2. **Deploy it yourself** if you have cluster-admin access:
   ```bash
   kubectl create namespace argocd
   kubectl apply -n argocd -f https://raw.githubusercontent.com/argoproj/argo-cd/stable/manifests/install.yaml
   ```

Until ArgoCD is running, you can deploy the Helm chart directly as a workaround:
```bash
helm install crewai-platform oci://registry.crewai.com/crewai/stable \
  --version 0.3.10 \
  --namespace crewai-dev \
  -f environments/dev/helm/values.yaml
```

+### GitHub Secrets
+
+The following secrets must be configured in GitHub for CI/CD workflows to function. Secrets can be set at the **repository level** (applies to all environments) or as **environment-scoped secrets** (per GitHub Environment) for environment-specific credentials.
+
+**Settings → Secrets and variables → Actions**
+
+#### Repository Secrets (all environments)
+
+| Secret | Used By | Description |
+|--------|---------|-------------|
+| `GCP_WORKLOAD_IDENTITY_PROVIDER` | terraform-plan, terraform-apply, terraform-drift | GCP Workload Identity Federation provider ID (e.g. `projects/PROJECT_NUMBER/locations/global/workloadIdentityPools/POOL/providers/PROVIDER`). Set up via the `github-actions-oidc` module in [kix-platform-infrastructure](https://github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-infrastructure). |
+| `GCP_SERVICE_ACCOUNT` | terraform-plan, terraform-apply, terraform-drift | GCP service account email for GitHub Actions (e.g. `github-kix-services-crewai-dev@kd-ix-eur-dev-gke.iam.gserviceaccount.com`). |
+| `TERRAFORM_MODULES_APP_ID` | terraform-plan, terraform-apply, terraform-drift, terraform-validate | GitHub App ID for accessing private Terraform modules in [kix-platform-modules](https://github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-modules). |
+| `TERRAFORM_MODULES_APP_PRIVATE_KEY` | terraform-plan, terraform-apply, terraform-drift, terraform-validate | GitHub App private key (PEM) for the above App. |
+
+> **Note:** If you need different GCP credentials per environment (e.g. separate workload identity pools for dev/qa/prod), configure `GCP_WORKLOAD_IDENTITY_PROVIDER` and `GCP_SERVICE_ACCOUNT` as environment-scoped secrets on the `dev`, `qa`, and `prod` GitHub Environments instead of repository-level secrets.
+
+#### Terraform Variables (sensitive inputs)
+
+These are passed to Terraform at plan/apply time via `TF_VAR_*` environment variables. They should be stored as GitHub Actions secrets and need to be added to the terraform-plan and terraform-apply workflows.
+
+| Secret | Terraform Variable | Description |
+|--------|--------------------|-------------|
+| `TF_VAR_oauth_client_id` | `oauth_client_id` | WorkOS OAuth client ID |
+| `TF_VAR_oauth_api_key` | `oauth_api_key` | WorkOS OAuth API key |
+| `TF_VAR_oauth_domain` | `oauth_domain` | WorkOS OAuth domain |
+| `TF_VAR_helm_registry_token` | `helm_registry_token` | CrewAI Helm OCI registry (`registry.crewai.com`) authentication token |
+
+> **Note:** These are currently **not wired** into the GitHub Actions workflows. They must be added as `env:` blocks on the Terraform Init/Plan/Apply steps, or set as environment-scoped secrets with `TF_VAR_` prefix so Terraform picks them up automatically.
+
+#### GitHub Environments (for approval gates)
+
+The `validate-plan-apply` workflow uses GitHub Environment protection rules for deployment approvals. Create these environments in **Settings → Environments**:
+
+| Environment | Purpose |
+|-------------|---------|
+| `dev-approval` | Approval gate before applying to dev |
+| `qa-approval` | Approval gate before applying to QA |
+| `prod-approval` | Approval gate before applying to production |
+
 ## CI/CD Pipeline
 
-This repository uses **GitHub Actions** for continuous integration and deployment with GitOps practices.
+This repository uses **GitHub Actions** for continuous integration and deployment.
 
 ### Automated Workflows
 
-1. **Terraform Plan** - Validates infrastructure changes on PRs
-   - Runs on: Pull requests modifying `terraform/**`
-   - Outputs: Terraform plan, security scan results
+| Workflow | Trigger | Description |
+|----------|---------|-------------|
+| **Auto Validate and Plan** | PR to `main`/`develop` modifying `environments/*/terraform/**` | Detects changed Terraform directories, runs validation + security scan + plan |
+| **Validate, Plan and Apply** | Manual (`workflow_dispatch`) | Full deploy pipeline: validate → plan → approval gate → apply |
+| **Validate and Plan** | Manual / called by nightly | Validate + plan for a single environment (no apply) |
+| **Nightly Quality Checks** | Schedule (02:00 UTC daily) | Validates develop→dev, main→qa and runs drift detection on all environments |
 
-2. **Terraform Apply** - Deploys infrastructure changes
-   - Runs on: Push to `main` branch
-   - Progression: Dev → QA → Production (with approvals)
+### Reusable Workflows
 
-3. **ArgoCD Sync** - Deploys application via GitOps
-   - Runs on: Push to `main` branch (helm changes)
-   - Progression: Dev → QA → Production (with approvals)
+| Workflow | Description |
+|----------|-------------|
+| `terraform-validate.yml` | Terraform fmt, validate, and Trivy security scan |
+| `terraform-plan.yml` | Terraform init + plan with statistics extraction |
+| `terraform-apply.yml` | Terraform apply with pre/post validation |
+| `terraform-drift.yml` | Drift detection with GitHub issue creation |
 
-4. **Promote Release** - Promotes tested versions between environments
-   - Runs on: Manual trigger
-   - Valid paths: Dev → QA, QA → Production
+### Branch-Environment Rules
 
-5. **Health Checks** - Monitors application health
-   - Runs on: Schedule (every 15 minutes)
+| Branch | Allowed Environments |
+|--------|---------------------|
+| `develop` | dev |
+| `main` | dev, qa |
+| `v*` tags | prod |
 
 ### Deployment Flow
 
 ```
-Developer → PR → Terraform Plan → Merge → Dev Deploy
+Developer → PR → Validate + Plan → Merge to develop
+                                            ↓
+                          Manual dispatch → Dev Apply (with approval)
+                                            ↓
+                          Manual dispatch → QA Apply (with approval)
                                             ↓
-                                    Promote → QA Deploy
-                                                  ↓
-                                    Approve → Production Deploy
+                        Tag release v* → Prod Apply (with approval, change window enforced)
 ```
 
 ## Quick Start

# Code Review Report: ArgoCD Application Manifests and Helm Values

## CRITICAL FINDINGS

### FINDING 1 [CRITICAL] -- ArgoCD Application Namespace Inconsistency (QA and Prod Not Updated)

The `metadata.namespace` field in the ArgoCD Application manifests is inconsistent across environments. Dev was recently fixed in commit `0b4bd94` to change from `argocd` to `crewai-root`, but QA and Prod were NOT updated.

| Environment | File | Line | Current Value | Expected Value |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| dev | `/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai/environments/dev/argocd/application.yaml` | Line 5 | `crewai-root` | `crewai-root` (CORRECT) |
| qa | `/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai/environments/qa/argocd/application.yaml` | Line 5 | `argocd` | `crewai-root` (WRONG) |
| prod | `/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai/environments/prod/argocd/application.yaml` | Line 5 | `argocd` | `crewai-root` (WRONG) |

The commit message for `0b4bd94` states: "Update dev ArgoCD Application namespace from 'argocd' to 'crewai-root' to match the actual ArgoCD installation on the cluster." If ArgoCD is installed in the `crewai-root` namespace across all clusters (which is the typical pattern for a shared platform), then QA and Prod will fail to sync because ArgoCD only watches for Application resources in its own installation namespace.

**Impact**: ArgoCD will not discover or manage the QA and Prod Application resources if they are created in the `argocd` namespace but ArgoCD is running in `crewai-root`.

---

### FINDING 2 [HIGH] -- Prod ArgoCD Application Missing Automated Sync Policy

The production ArgoCD Application is missing the `automated` block under `syncPolicy`, while dev and qa both have it.

**File**: `/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai/environments/prod/argocd/application.yaml`, Lines 32-43

```yaml
  syncPolicy:
    syncOptions:
      - CreateNamespace=true
      ...
```

Compare with dev (lines 31-36) and qa (lines 31-36):
```yaml
  syncPolicy:
    automated:
      prune: true
      selfHeal: true
      allowEmpty: false
    syncOptions:
      ...
```

**Assessment**: This is likely **intentional** for production -- requiring manual sync approval is a best practice for prod. However, this should be explicitly documented with a comment in the file. The absence of `automated` means deployments to prod require manual ArgoCD sync triggers. If this is intentional, add a comment. If it was accidentally omitted, it is a significant operational gap.

---

### FINDING 3 [HIGH] -- Prod Has Extra Notification Annotation (Inconsistency Review)

Production has an additional notification annotation that dev and qa do not:

**File**: `/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai/environments/prod/argocd/application.yaml`, Line 11
```yaml
    notifications.argoproj.io/subscribe.on-sync-failed.googlechat: "crewai-alerts"
```

Dev and QA only have `on-sync-succeeded` and `on-health-degraded`. This is **likely correct** (prod should alert on sync failures), but **dev and qa should arguably also have `on-sync-failed`** to catch deployment failures in lower environments before they reach prod.

---

| `POSTGRES_CABLE_DB` | `crewai_plus_cable_production` | `crewai_plus_cable_production` | `crewai_plus_cable_production` |
| `POSTGRES_OAUTH_DB` | `crewai_plus_oauth_db` | `crewai_plus_oauth_db` | `crewai_plus_oauth_db` |

```yaml

```

This is set to `"production"` across dev, qa, and prod. While this is a common Rails practice (to get production-like behavior including asset compilation and error handling), it means dev and qa cannot use Rails environment-specific configuration for debugging. This is worth noting but is likely intentional for this deployment model.

### FINDING 6 [MEDIUM] -- Hardcoded Chart Version Across All Environments

All three ArgoCD applications pin the same chart version:

- `/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai/environments/qa/argocd/application.yaml`, Line 17
- `/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai/environments/prod/argocd/application.yaml`, Line 17

```yaml
targetRevision: 0.3.10
```

All environments use `0.3.10`. For a promotion-based workflow, dev should typically run a newer version first before qa and prod. Having all three on the same version suggests either:
- Versions are bumped atomically across environments (risky for prod stability), or
- This was recently synchronized and the staggered promotion workflow is not yet in place.

**Recommendation**: Consider whether dev should track a newer or "latest" revision while prod stays pinned.

## LOW / INFORMATIONAL FINDINGS

### FINDING 8 [LOW] -- `CreateNamespace=true` Sync Option Is Redundant

All three ArgoCD applications have:
```yaml
syncOptions:
  - CreateNamespace=true
```

Since Terraform already creates the namespace (`crewai-{env}`) via the `gke_config` module with resource quotas, network policies, and RBAC, ArgoCD creating the namespace would override or conflict with Terraform-managed namespace configuration. The `CreateNamespace=true` is redundant at best and could be problematic if ArgoCD recreates a namespace that Terraform expects to manage (potentially losing labels, annotations, quotas).

**Files**: All three `application.yaml` files, at lines 37 (dev/qa) and 34 (prod).

### FINDING 9 [LOW] -- `destination.server` Uses In-Cluster URL for All Environments

All three environments use:
```yaml
destination:
  server: https://kubernetes.default.svc
```

This means each ArgoCD Application manifest must be applied to the correct cluster's ArgoCD instance. This is fine for a pattern where each cluster has its own ArgoCD, but it means a central ArgoCD managing multiple clusters would not work with these manifests. This is informational only -- the current pattern appears intentional.

### FINDING 10 [LOW] -- Dev/QA Missing `terminationGracePeriodSeconds` Parity

Dev sets `web.terminationGracePeriodSeconds: 30` (line 92) and prod sets it to `60` (line 92). QA also sets `30` (line 92). While the difference between dev/qa (30s) and prod (60s) is reasonable (prod needs more graceful shutdown time), this is worth documenting for operational awareness.

### FINDING 11 [LOW] -- Prod Missing `pod_security_standards` in Terraform (Dev and QA Also Missing)

Only the production Terraform `main.tf` includes `pod_security_standards` in the `gke_config` module call:

**File**: `/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai/environments/prod/terraform/main.tf`, Lines 290-296
```hcl
  pod_security_standards = {
    enabled             = true
    default_mode        = "enforce"
    default_level       = "baseline"
    namespace_overrides = {}
  }
```

Dev and QA `gke_config` module calls do NOT include this parameter. This means pod security standards enforcement may not be active in dev and qa, which reduces the ability to catch security issues before prod.

# Terraform / ArgoCD / Helm Cross-Configuration Review Report

## CRITICAL FINDINGS

### FINDING 1 [CRITICAL] -- ArgoCD Application Namespace Inconsistency Across Environments

**Files affected:**
- `/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai/environments/dev/argocd/application.yaml` -- line 5
- `/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai/environments/qa/argocd/application.yaml` -- line 5
- `/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai/environments/prod/argocd/application.yaml` -- line 5

**Details:**
The `metadata.namespace` for the ArgoCD Application resource is **different between dev and the other two environments**:

| Environment | namespace value |
|---|---|
| dev | `crewai-root` |
| qa | `argocd` |
| prod | `argocd` |

Dev uses:
```yaml
metadata:
  name: crewai-dev
  namespace: crewai-root
```

QA uses:
```yaml
metadata:
  name: crewai-qa
  namespace: argocd
```

Prod uses:
```yaml
metadata:
  name: crewai-prod
  namespace: argocd
```

**Impact:** This is the most critical finding. The ArgoCD Application CRD must be deployed into the namespace where ArgoCD's application controller is watching. If all three clusters use the same ArgoCD installation convention, then one of these is wrong. Either dev should be `argocd` to match QA/prod, or QA/prod should be `crewai-root` to match dev. This mismatch will cause ArgoCD to fail to pick up and sync the application in whichever environment has the incorrect namespace.

**Assessment:** Based on the fact that `crewai-root` appears to be a project-specific ArgoCD namespace pattern (potentially using ArgoCD's app-of-apps pattern with a dedicated namespace), and that dev was updated most recently (the latest commit message says "updated workflows"), it is likely that `crewai-root` is the correct value and QA/prod were not updated. However, this must be confirmed with the team.

---

### FINDING 2 [HIGH] -- Duplicate Workload Identity Binding (Terraform Inline vs. gke-config Module)

**Files affected:**
- `/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai/environments/dev/terraform/main.tf` -- lines 214-218
- `/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai/environments/qa/terraform/main.tf` -- lines 182-186
- `/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai/environments/prod/terraform/main.tf` -- lines 182-186

**Details:**
Each environment's `main.tf` has an **inline** `google_service_account_iam_member.workload_identity` resource:

```hcl
resource "google_service_account_iam_member" "workload_identity" {
  service_account_id = google_service_account.crewai_platform.name
  role               = "roles/iam.workloadIdentityUser"
  member             = "serviceAccount:${var.gke_project_id}.svc.id.goog[crewai-${var.environment}/crewai-platform-sa]"
}
```

However, the `gke_config` module **also** creates a workload identity binding internally (file: `.terraform/modules/gke_config/terraform/kubernetes/gke-config/service-accounts.tf`, lines 37-49):

```hcl
resource "google_service_account_iam_member" "workload_identity" {
  for_each = {
    for ns_name, sa_config in local.service_accounts :
    ns_name => sa_config
    if sa_config.gcp_service_account != null
  }
  service_account_id = "projects/${var.project_id}/serviceAccounts/${each.value.gcp_service_account}"
  role               = "roles/iam.workloadIdentityUser"
  member             = "serviceAccount:${var.project_id}.svc.id.goog[${each.key}/${coalesce(each.value.name, "${each.key}-sa")}]"
}
```

Since the `crewai-${var.environment}` namespace config passes `gcp_service_account = google_service_account.crewai_platform.email`, the module will also attempt to create this same IAM binding. This results in a **duplicate** IAM binding for the `crewai-${env}` namespace's service account. While GCP IAM is additive and this may not cause a hard error, it creates:
- Terraform state confusion (two resources managing the same binding)
- Risk of drift if one is modified but not the other
- Potential `terraform apply` failures due to conflicting state

Note: The `crewai-crews-${var.environment}` namespace does NOT have `gcp_service_account` set, so it does not have a duplicate binding. But it also means the crews service account has no GCP IAM binding at all -- which may or may not be intentional.

---

### FINDING 3 [HIGH] -- Database Names Are Identical Across All Environments

**Files affected:**
- `/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai/environments/dev/terraform/main.tf` -- lines 37-56
- `/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai/environments/qa/terraform/main.tf` -- lines 25-44
- `/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai/environments/prod/terraform/main.tf` -- lines 25-44

**Details:**
All three environments create databases with identical hardcoded names:
```hcl
name = "crewai_plus_production"
name = "crewai_plus_cable_production"
name = "crewai_plus_oauth_db"
```

These names contain `production` even in dev and QA environments. While each environment uses a separate Cloud SQL instance (the instance name is environment-specific via `var.cloud_sql_instance_name`), the database names themselves are confusing and could lead to operational mistakes. If someone connects to the dev Cloud SQL instance, they would see databases named `crewai_plus_production`, which is misleading.

Similarly, the `secret_db_credentials` module in all environments stores:
```hcl
database = "crewai_plus_production"
username = "crewai"
```

And the Helm values in all environments reference:
```yaml
POSTGRES_DB: "crewai_plus_production"
```

This is internally consistent (Terraform creates the DB, Helm references it), but the naming is semantically incorrect for non-production environments.

### FINDING 4 [MEDIUM] -- Pod Security Standards Only Configured for Prod

**Files affected:**
- `/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai/environments/dev/terraform/main.tf` -- `gke_config` module (lines 246-325): no `pod_security_standards` block
- `/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai/environments/qa/terraform/main.tf` -- `gke_config` module (lines 210-289): no `pod_security_standards` block
- `/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai/environments/prod/terraform/main.tf` -- `gke_config` module (lines 210-296): includes `pod_security_standards`

**Details:**
Only the prod environment configures `pod_security_standards`:
```hcl
pod_security_standards = {
  enabled             = true
  default_mode        = "enforce"
  default_level       = "baseline"
  namespace_overrides = {}
}
```

Dev and QA do not set this variable at all. Per the gke-config module's variables.tf, the default is `{}` which results in `enabled = true`, `default_mode = "enforce"`, `default_level = "baseline"`. So the behavior may be the same via defaults, but this is inconsistent. If the intent is to NOT enforce PSS in dev/qa, it should be explicitly `enabled = false`. If the intent is the same across all environments, it should be explicit everywhere for clarity.

**Files affected:**
- `/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai/environments/dev/terraform/main.tf` -- lines 224-232
- `/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai/environments/qa/terraform/main.tf` -- lines 192-200
- `/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai/environments/prod/terraform/main.tf` -- lines 192-200

**Details:**
The Terraform resource is named `crewai_builder`:
```hcl
resource "google_artifact_registry_repository" "crewai_builder" {
```

But the actual `repository_id` is:
```hcl
repository_id = "crewai-${var.environment}"
```

The resource name suggests it is specifically for the "builder" component, but the repository ID is generic (`crewai-dev`, `crewai-qa`, `crewai-prod`). This is a minor naming inconsistency between the Terraform resource name and the actual GCP resource. The Terraform resource identifier (`crewai_builder`) implies a specialized purpose, while the GCP repository name (`crewai-${env}`) is generic. This is consistent across all three environments, so there is no cross-environment mismatch, but it could be confusing if additional Artifact Registry repositories are needed later.

### FINDING 7 [MEDIUM] -- AWS_BUCKET Values in Helm May Not Match Actual GCS Bucket Names

**Files affected:**
- `/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai/environments/dev/helm/values.yaml` -- line 213
- `/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai/environments/qa/helm/values.yaml` -- line 213
- `/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai/environments/prod/helm/values.yaml` -- line 253

**Details:**
The Helm values hardcode:
```yaml
AWS_BUCKET: "crewai-dev-data"   # dev
AWS_BUCKET: "crewai-qa-data"    # qa
AWS_BUCKET: "crewai-prod-data"  # prod
```

However, the actual GCS bucket names are generated by the remote `gcs` module:
```hcl
module "gcs_data" {
  source      = "github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-modules//terraform/gcp/data-services/gcs?ref=main"
  project_id  = var.gke_project_id
  environment = var.environment
  use_case    = "crewai"
  name_suffix = "data"
  cost_center = "platform"
  location    = var.region
}
```

The Terraform output `data_bucket_name` exposes `module.gcs_data.bucket_name`, but we cannot see the bucket naming convention inside the remote module. If the GCS module uses a naming pattern like `${project_id}-${use_case}-${environment}-${name_suffix}` (e.g., `kd-ix-eur-dev-gke-crewai-dev-data`), it would NOT match the hardcoded `crewai-dev-data` in Helm values. This is a potential runtime failure where the application cannot find its storage bucket.

**Recommendation:** The `AWS_BUCKET` value should either be derived from Terraform outputs (passed through a ConfigMap or ExternalSecret) or the naming convention of the GCS module should be verified to match.

---

### FINDING 8 [LOW] -- Cloud SQL Proxy Connection String Hardcoded in Helm Values

**Files affected:**
- `/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai/environments/dev/helm/values.yaml` -- line 243
- `/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai/environments/qa/helm/values.yaml` -- line 243
- `/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai/environments/prod/helm/values.yaml` -- line 317

**Details:**
The Cloud SQL Proxy connection strings are hardcoded:
```yaml
# dev
- "kd-ix-eur-dev-platform-data:europe-west1:kd-ix-eur-dev-cloudsql-platform"
# qa
- "kd-ix-eur-qa-platform-data:europe-west1:kd-ix-eur-qa-cloudsql-platform"
# prod
- "kd-ix-eur-prod-platform-data:europe-west1:kd-ix-eur-prod-cloudsql-platform"
```

These are composed from `${platform_data_project}:${region}:${cloud_sql_instance_name}`. Terraform outputs the `cloud_sql_connection_name`, which provides exactly this value. If the Cloud SQL instance or project names ever change, these Helm values would need manual updates. This is a maintenance risk but not a current mismatch -- the hardcoded values correctly correspond to the Terraform variables:
- dev: `platform_data_project=kd-ix-eur-dev-platform-data`, `cloud_sql_instance_name=kd-ix-eur-dev-cloudsql-platform`
- qa: `platform_data_project=kd-ix-eur-qa-platform-data`, `cloud_sql_instance_name=kd-ix-eur-qa-cloudsql-platform`
- prod: `platform_data_project=kd-ix-eur-prod-platform-data`, `cloud_sql_instance_name=kd-ix-eur-prod-cloudsql-platform`

These all match. But they are fragile.

### FINDING 9 [LOW] -- All Remote Module Sources Pinned to `ref=main` (Not Versioned)

**Files affected:** All `main.tf` files across all three environments.

**Details:**
Every module source uses `?ref=main`:
```hcl
source = "github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-modules//terraform/gcp/data-services/gcs?ref=main"
source = "github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-modules//terraform/gcp/secret-manager?ref=main"
source = "github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-modules//terraform/kubernetes/gke-config?ref=main"
```

This means all three environments (including prod) will pull whatever is currently on `main` of the platform modules repo. Per the module repo's own CLAUDE.md standards, production should use pinned versions:
```hcl
# Development (latest)
source = "...?ref=main"
# Production (pinned)
source = "...?ref=v1.0.0"
```

A breaking change merged to `main` in the platform-modules repo would affect all environments simultaneously.

### FINDING 10 [LOW] -- Kubernetes Secret and Service Account Name Cross-Reference (VERIFIED CONSISTENT)

This is a positive finding -- the following names are consistently referenced across Terraform and Helm:

| Resource | Terraform creates | Helm references |
|---|---|---|
| K8s Secret (credentials) | `crewai-credentials` (in ns `crewai-${env}`) | `envFrom.secretRef.name: "crewai-credentials"` |
| K8s Secret (registry) | `crewai-registry-secret` (in ns `crewai-${env}`) | `global.imagePullSecrets[0].name: "crewai-registry-secret"` |
| K8s ServiceAccount | `crewai-platform-sa` (in ns `crewai-${env}`) | `serviceAccount.name: "crewai-platform-sa"`, `serviceAccount.create: false` |
| Destination namespace | `crewai-${env}` (via gke_config) | ArgoCD `destination.namespace: crewai-${env}` |
| Crew namespace | `crewai-crews-${env}` (via gke_config) | Helm `crewNamespace: "crewai-crews-${env}"` |

All of these are correctly aligned. The Helm charts reference `serviceAccount.create: false` which means they will use the service account created by Terraform rather than creating their own -- this is correct.

### FINDING 11 [INFO] -- Network Policy for crewai-crews Namespace Correctly References Parent Namespace

**Files affected:** All three `main.tf` files.

**Details:**
The `crewai-crews-${var.environment}` namespace has:
```hcl
network_isolation = {
  allow_from_namespaces = ["crewai-${var.environment}"]
  ...
}
```

This correctly allows ingress from the platform namespace into the crew workloads namespace. The gke-config module's network-policies.tf (line 183-212) uses `kubernetes.io/metadata.name` label selector for namespace matching, which is automatically set by Kubernetes on all namespaces. This is correct.

The quotas scale sensibly:

This is a reasonable scaling pattern.

> AGENT

The ArgoCD Application manifest itself will apply fine to the `crewai-root` namespace, but the Helm chart it deploys will almost certainly **fail to become healthy**. Terraform provisions the dependencies the app needs:

| Dependency | Created by Terraform | What happens without it |
|---|---|---|
| `crewai-registry-secret` | K8s secret (dockerconfigjson) | Image pulls from `registry.crewai.com` fail — pods stuck in `ImagePullBackOff` |
| `crewai-credentials` | K8s secret (DB, GCS, OAuth creds) | Pods crash on startup — missing env vars / secret mounts |
| Cloud SQL databases | `google_sql_database` resources | App can't connect to its database |
| GCS buckets | `data-services/gcs` module | No storage for data/logs |
| `crewai-platform-sa` | K8s service account + Workload Identity | Pods can't authenticate to GCP services |
| Cloud SQL Proxy | Needs SA + Cloud SQL instance | Sidecar container crash-loops |

ArgoCD will keep retrying (you have `retry.limit: 5` with backoff), and with `selfHeal: true` it'll keep attempting to reconcile. So you'll see the Application in a **Degraded** state with pods in `ImagePullBackOff` or `CrashLoopBackOff`.

**Recommended order:**
1. Run `terraform apply` first to create all infrastructure and secrets
2. Then apply the ArgoCD manifest (or let it auto-sync if already applied)

If you do apply ArgoCD first, it won't cause any damage — it'll just sit in a degraded state until the Terraform resources exist, then self-heal on the next sync cycle.

> DEVELOPER

how do i dynamically select the destination server, and does the namespace need to exist alteady?

> AGENT

**Destination server:**

Currently all three environments use `https://kubernetes.default.svc`, which means "deploy to the same cluster ArgoCD is running on." This works if ArgoCD is installed on each GKE cluster individually.

If ArgoCD runs on **one cluster** and deploys to the others, you have two options:

1. **Register remote clusters** with ArgoCD, then reference by server URL:
   ```bash
   # Register each cluster context
   argocd cluster add gke_kd-ix-eur-dev-gke_europe-west1_kd-ix-eur-dev-gke
   argocd cluster add gke_kd-ix-eur-qa-gke_europe-west1_kd-ix-eur-qa-gke
   ```
   Then use the cluster's API endpoint in the manifest:
   ```yaml
   destination:
     server: https://<cluster-api-endpoint>
     namespace: crewai-dev
   ```

2. **Reference by cluster name** (cleaner):
   ```yaml
   destination:
     name: kd-ix-eur-dev-gke    # matches the name ArgoCD knows it by
     namespace: crewai-dev
   ```

You can check what clusters ArgoCD knows about with:
```bash
argocd cluster list
# or
kubectl get secrets -n crewai-root -l argocd.argoproj.io/secret-type=cluster
```

If `https://kubernetes.default.svc` is the only registered cluster and ArgoCD lives on the same cluster as the target environment, what you have is correct.

**Namespace existence:**

No, it does **not** need to exist already. You have `CreateNamespace=true` in your syncOptions, which tells ArgoCD to create it if missing. However, the namespace ArgoCD creates will be bare — no resource quotas, network policies, or labels. Since your Terraform also creates these namespaces with quotas and policies, the cleaner path is:

1. **Terraform first** — creates namespace with quotas, network policies, service accounts
2. **ArgoCD second** — finds namespace already exists, deploys into it

If ArgoCD runs first, it creates a bare namespace, then Terraform will reconcile it on the next apply (adding quotas/policies). No conflict, just a brief window without guardrails.
